## Coverage
The monthly credit applies to:
- "Claude Agent SDK usage in your own projects (Python or TypeScript)"
- "The `claude -p` command in Claude Code (non-interactive mode)"
- "The Claude Code GitHub Actions integration"

- **Team (Premium seats)**: $100

# Agent SDK overview

> Build production AI agents with Claude Code as a library

An agent is an application that completes a task by planning its own steps and calling tools that read files, run commands, or edit code. The Agent SDK gives you the same tools, [agent loop](/docs/en/agent-sdk/agent-loop), and context management that power Claude Code, programmable in Python and TypeScript.

The deciding factors are simplicity, restart behaviour, explainability, and a clean path to production.\n\n### Options compared\n\n| Option | Type | For | Against |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **In-memory numpy (custom)** | In-memory | No extra dependency or container, exact results, about 60 lines of fully explainable code, trivial to unit test, fastest for small corpora | Lost on restart, so embeddings must be recomputed (slow on CPU). No metadata filtering or ANN index. Single-process only |\n| ChromaDB (embedded, `PersistentClient`) | Local, on disk | Persistence, metadata filters, suggested in the brief, runs in-process | Heavy dependency tree, frequent breaking API changes, and a black box to explain. Brings no benefit at ~100 chunks |\n| ChromaDB (server container) | Local service | Same as above, plus a separate service | An extra container and network hop for nothing at this size |\n| pgvector (Postgres in Docker) | Local service | Productio

### 🤖 Claude

I've finished all three steps in `steps.md` and added the findings to the task files. I also ticked the three research items in the README checklist. I haven't committed anything.

**Step 1: `goal.md`.** It only states the goal: a RAG chatbot that answers CSMs' questions with citations, using only internal documentation. There was nothing to do beyond reading it.

**Step 2: `research.md`.** The findings and reasoning are appended to the file.
- **Backend: Python with FastAPI.** Python has the best RAG and vector-maths tools, the brief recommends it, and FastAPI generates the API docs for free. I suggest calling the model SDKs directly instead of using LangChain, so every block of code is easy to explain in the interview.
- **Frontend: Vite + React + TypeScript + TailwindCSS.** Next.js would duplicate the backend we already have.
- **Local model:** your machine has 4 CPU cores, 15 GB RAM and no GPU, and Ollama isn't installed yet. So the model has to be about 4B parameters or smaller:
  - Chat model: `gemma3:4b`, with `qwen3:4b` as the alternative to compare against.
  - Embedding model: `embeddinggemma`, which supports many languages, so it covers your planned multi-language feature.
  - Anthropic has no embeddings API, so embeddings always run locally in Ollama, even when Claude answers the questions.
- **Claude:**
  - **Main option:** your own API key in `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, which is what the brief asks for.
  - **Subscription:** since June 2026, Pro and Max plans include a monthly credit for Claude's agent library (the Agent SDK). But Anthropic doesn't allow apps to offer a "log in with your Claude subscription" option to their users. The subscription can only be an optional setup for your own personal testing.

**Step 3: `storage.md`.**
- **In-memory vector store.** The 5 articles come to about 100 chunks, so a simple exact search in memory takes microseconds.
- **Saved to disk.** The vectors are saved so a restart doesn't recompute them on a slow CPU. They are rebuilt automatically when a document or the embedding model changes.
- **Easy to swap later.** The store sits behind a small interface, so pgvector can replace it if the project grows.
- **Refusal threshold.** A minimum similarity score decides when the documentation doesn't cover a question. That supports your polite-refusal / hand-off-to-a-human item.
- **Articles.** I also proposed chunking by Markdown heading and 5 topics for the mock articles.

**Things that weren't clear:**
1. `research.md` compares options that aren't really alternatives. React vs Next.js vs Vite: Vite is the build tool that runs React. TypeScript vs Tailwind: one is the language, the other is the styling, and the recommendation is to use both.
2. "Claude with subscription": do you want the optional subscription setup for your own testing, or is the API key enough?
3. The README recommends C#/.NET, but the task only compared Python and Node, so I left .NET out.

**Questions before the next phase:**
- Are the 5 proposed article topics OK?
- Should I install Ollama locally, or only run it through Docker Compose?
- Should I commit these documentation changes?

### 🤖 Claude

The design now leaves room for all 7 ideas without building any of them. I added a "Future-readiness design" section to `ideas.md` that maps each idea to the parts that must exist from the start and the parts that come later. None of the ideas are marked done, since none are implemented yet.

**What the first version will include, because it's cheap now and saves refactoring later:**
- **Full result for every chat turn:** besides the answer and citations, each response carries the provider, model, token usage, timings, the best relevance score and whether it refused. For now this is only logged; the stats, costs, history and tests tabs will later store and read it.
- **An API that can grow:** the routes are versioned (`/api/v1/...`), and requests take an optional `options` field for language, detail level and model choice. Every response has a conversation id and a message id, which history will need.
- **Chat models and embeddings behind separate, configurable interfaces.** This matters for idea #7 ("Claude only, no Ollama"). Anthropic has no embeddings API, so turning Ollama off means the embeddings have to come from somewhere else. That can be the same `embeddinggemma` model running inside the backend, or Voyage AI. Ollama becomes an optional part of the Docker Compose setup.
- **Prompts as template files,** so the concise and detailed styles (idea #3) are just two templates.
- **A test question set stored as a data file,** with the evaluation code in its own module. pytest uses it now, and a hidden tests tab can run it later without starting pytest from the web.

**Left for later:**
- A SQLite database for stats, costs, history and settings. I've added it as a note in `storage.md`, kept separate from the vector index, which can always be rebuilt.
- The admin endpoints, protected by an `ADMIN_TOKEN` setting. Hiding a menu doesn't make it secure, so the token is what protects the data.
- The hidden admin screens in the frontend.

I also added a short note to `research.md` about the embeddings change and a planned project structure. `CLAUDE.md` now says "keep the extension points, don't implement the ideas", and I saved the same rule as a memory for future sessions. Nothing is committed yet.

### 🤖 Claude

I've added a 10-phase build plan (Phases 0–9) to `documentation/taskdocs/steps.md`, and it's ready for you to check. I also marked the four task-doc steps (goal, research, storage, ideas) as ✅. I haven't started any code and haven't committed anything.

**The phases**
0. **Project skeleton and tools:** folders, uv/ruff/pytest, `.env.example`, and the build and test commands added to `CLAUDE.md`.
1. **Mock knowledge base:** the 5 OmniCorp articles, plus about 15 test questions with their expected source documents. About 3 of them are questions the docs can't answer, to check that the bot refuses.
2. **Configuration and model connections:** separate, switchable connections for Ollama and Claude (the Anthropic API), with chat models and embeddings kept apart.
3. **Loading the documents:** splitting them into chunks, the in-memory vector store, and saving it to disk. Target: the right document appears in the search results for at least 80% of the test questions.
4. **Question-answering pipeline and prompts:** citations, refusing politely when the answer isn't in the docs, and the full result for every chat turn.
5. **Backend API:** `/api/v1` with a normal JSON chat endpoint and a streaming one (SSE), plus health and provider-status checks.
6. **Frontend:** chat screen with streamed answers and citation cards, plus an empty `/admin` page reserved for the future hidden tabs.
7. **Docker Compose:** one setup that runs everything locally, including Ollama, and one that uses Claude with an API key.
8. **Tests and measurements:** checks on search accuracy, refusals and response speed, plus optional CI.
9. **README and AI logs:** the README sections the brief requires, and the Claude transcripts exported to Markdown (mandatory).

Each phase has **"Done when"** checks. After each phase I'll mark it ✅, commit, and **stop for your review**.

**Please confirm or change these before I start Phase 0:**
1. **Phase 1 content:** are the 5 article topics OK? You'll review the articles themselves at the end of Phase 1.
2. **Ollama:** should I install it on this machine for development, or run it only through Docker?
3. **Git:** may I commit the documentation changes made so far (`CLAUDE.md` and the task docs), and should I work directly on `main`?
4. **CI:** do you want the GitHub Actions workflow in Phase 8, or should I skip it?

This reference describes the limits for each plan, how clients should react when limits are reached, and how customers can request higher limits.\n\n## Limits per plan\n\nRate limits are applied **per workspace**, not per API token. All tokens of a workspace share the same budget.\n\n| Plan | Requests per minute | Daily quota | Burst |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Starter | 60 | 10,000 | up to 120 requests for 10 seconds |\n| Business | 600 | 250,000 | up to 1,200 requests for 10 seconds |\n| Enterprise | 3,000 | 2,000,000 by default (custom quotas available) | up to 6,000 requests for 10 seconds |\n\nThe daily quota resets at **00:00 UTC**.\n\n### How the burst works\n\nLimits use a token-bucket algorithm. A workspace can briefly exceed its per-minute limit by up to **2×** for a maximum of **10 seconds**, after which requests are throttled back to the sustained rate. CSMs should use it to answer customer security and compliance questionnaires.\n\n## Data retention\n\n### Active workspace data\n\nRecords, files and comments are kept for as long as the customer's subscription is active. Items deleted by users go to the **Trash**, where they can be restored for **30 days**. After 30 days they are permanently deleted from live systems.\n\n### Audit logs\n\n| Plan | Audit log retention |\n|---|---|\n| Starter | 90 days |\n| Business | 1 year |\n| Enterprise | 7 years (configurable between 1 and 7 years) |\n\nAudit logs can be streamed to the customer's SIEM using webhooks or exported as CSV.\n\n### After contract termination\n\n1. The workspace becomes **read-only** for a **30-day grace period**. During this time Admins can export all data.\n2. After the grace period, the workspace and its data are deleted from live systems **within 60 days**.\n3. Copies in backups are purged as backups expire, **no later than 35 days**

This manual covers configuration, security, delivery guarantees and troubleshooting.\n\n## Webhooks\n\n### Creating an endpoint\n\nAdmins create webhook endpoints in **Admin Console → Integrations → Webhooks → Add endpoint**:\n\n1. Enter the HTTPS URL of the receiver. Plain HTTP URLs and private IP ranges are rejected.\n2. Select the event types to subscribe to.\n3. Save. The **signing secret** is displayed **once**; the customer must store it securely.\n\nA workspace can have up to **25 endpoints** on Business and up to **100 endpoints** on Enterprise. ### 🤖 Claude

Phase 1 content is written: 5 knowledge base articles and a set of 18 test questions. It only counts as done once you've reviewed it, so it's marked 🔍 (awaiting review) in `steps.md`. I checked that the files parse and that every expected document ID exists; the retrieval checks themselves run in Phase 3.

**The 5 articles** are in `backend/data/kb/`, about 750–920 words each. Each has front matter (`id`, `title`, `product`, `lang`, `updated`, `tags`) and `##`/`###` sections that the chunker will use.

| ID | Title | Main facts |
|---|---|---|
| kb-001 | SSO & User Provisioning Guide | SAML/OIDC/SCIM by plan, role mapping, break-glass accounts, `SSO-4xx` error table |
| kb-002 | API Rate Limits & Quotas Reference | 60 / 600 / 3,000 requests per minute by plan, burst, headers, 429 backoff, how to raise limits |
| kb-003 | Data Retention, Backup & GDPR Policy | Trash 30 days, backups 35 days, RPO/RTO, data regions (EU/US/APAC), GDPR requests |
| kb-004 | Webhooks & Integrations Manual | Events, HMAC signatures, 8 retries and auto-disable, Salesforce and Slack connectors |
| kb-005 | Support Tiers, SLAs & Escalation | P1–P4 priorities, response times, uptime credits, escalation path, **handing off to a human when the KB has no answer** |

The articles point to each other (for example, an SSO lockout is a P1 ticket, and Salesforce syncs use up the API rate limit). The facts are consistent across all five, which I checked by hand, so questions that need two documents have one correct answer.

**The questions** are in `backend/tests/eval/questions.yaml`:
- 10 questions answered by a single document.
- 4 questions that need two documents.
- 1 question in German, to test multi-language search over the English docs.
- 3 questions the docs don't answer: pricing, LDAP, and the mobile app's offline mode. The bot must refuse politely on these.
- Each question lists its expected documents and the key facts a correct answer should contain.

**Please review:**
1. Is the content realistic enough, and is anything you want missing?
2. Some questions are deliberately tricky. For example, q13 asks about data deleted two months ago. The correct answer is "no longer restorable", because the Trash keeps items for 30 days and backups for 35.

**Still open:**
- Which branch to commit to (nothing is committed yet).
- Whether to install Ollama on this machine or run it only in Docker. Phase 3 needs it to get the real embeddings and tune `MIN_SCORE`.

# Streaming - Python

## Quick Start

```python
with client.messages.stream(
    model="claude-opus-5",
    max_tokens=64000,
    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Write a story"}]
) as stream:
    for text in stream.text_stream:
        print(text, end="", flush=True)
```

### Async

```python
async with async_client.messages.stream(
    model="claude-opus-5",
    max_tokens=64000,
    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Write a story"}]
) as stream:
    async for text in stream.text_stream:
        print(text, end="", flush=True)
```

### Low-level: `stream=True`

`messages.stream()` (above) is the recommended helper - it accumulates state and exposes `text_stream` / `get_final_message()`. If you only need the raw event iterator and want lower memory use, pass `stream=True` to `messages.create()` instead:

```python
for event in client.messages.create(
    model="claude-opus-5",
    max_tokens=64000,
    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Write a story"}],
    stream=True,
):
    print(event.type)
```

No final-message accumulation is done for you in this form.

---

## Handling Different Content Types

Claude may return text, thinking blocks, or tool use. Handle each appropriately:

441:### Refusal Fallbacks (Claude Fable 5.1) - opt in by default
442-
443-Fallbacks are **opt-in**: without them a refused request simply stops. Include the server-side `fallbacks` parameter in `claude-fable-5-1` code by default - on a policy decline the API re-runs the same request on the fallback model inside the same call. A mid-stream decline is billed at normal rates, and the rescue bills at the fallback model's own rates, with cache repricing applied automatically; for a decline before any output, see [How refusals are billed](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/refusals-and-fallback#how-refusals-are-billed).

452-)

985:**1. `fallbacks: "default"` - recommended for every caller.** Claude Opus 5's safety classifiers can decline a request; the `fallbacks` parameter re-runs a declined request on another model server-side instead of returning the refusal to you. Previously you named the substitute yourself (`"fallbacks": [{"model": "claude-opus-4-8"}]`). The new `"default"` mode picks Anthropic's recommended fallback automatically, routed **by refusal category** - cyber-category refusals go to Claude Opus 4.8.
991:{"model": "claude-opus-5", "fallbacks": "default", "max_tokens": 1024,
995:**Prefer `"default"` over pinning a model.** Different fallback models carry different classifiers, so the right substitute depends on *why* the request was declined - and `"default"` removes the migration you would otherwise owe when a pinned fallback model is deprecated. Note the header is `server-side-fallback-2026-07-01`, distinct from the `-2026-06-01` header that gates the array form; the array form's semantics (content blocks, `usage.iterations`, sticky routing) are unchanged and documented in the Claude Fable 5.1 refusal section below.
1154:- [ ] **[TUNE]** Handle `stop_reason: "refusal"` before reading `content`, and opt into `fallbacks: "default"` (`server-side-fallback-2026-07-01`) rather than pinning a model - cyber-category refusals route to Claude Opus 4.8
1410:- **Header depends on the form you use.** The **array** form (`fallbacks: [{...}]`) requires exactly `server-side-fallback-2026-06-01`

  "content": "\"\"\"Provider-independent errors. The API layer maps these to HTTP status codes.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass ProviderError(Exception):\n    \"\"\"A provider failed in a way that retrying will not fix (bad request, unknown model...).\"\"\"\n\n\nclass ProviderUnavailableError(ProviderError):\n    \"\"\"The provider is unreachable, overloaded or rate-limited; retrying later may succeed.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass ProviderConfigError(ProviderError):\n    \"\"\"The provider is misconfigured, e.g. a missing or invalid API key.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass ProviderDisabledError(ProviderError):\n    \"\"\"The provider exists but is not listed in ENABLED_LLM_PROVIDERS.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass UnknownProviderError(ProviderError):\n    \"\"\"No provider with this name is registered.\"\"\"\n"
}

### 🤖 Claude

The Docker setup works, and Phase 2 is done and committed on the new `dev` branch. Following the plan, I'm stopping here for your review before Phase 3. There's also a speed problem you need to decide on first (point 3).

**1. Docker Compose setup: working.** `docker compose up -d --build` starts four services:
- **ollama:** the model server. On first run, a one-off helper (`ollama-init`) downloads `gemma3:4b` and `embeddinggemma`.
- **backend:** FastAPI with a health check at `/api/v1/health`.
- **frontend:** a placeholder React page (no chat yet), served by nginx, which forwards `/api` calls to the backend. It shows the backend status at http://localhost:8080.

I checked all the connections in the browser path, and `/admin` also loads the app. To make Compose work I built the health endpoint (planned for Phase 5), the frontend skeleton (Phase 6) and most of Phase 7 early. Phase 7 is marked as partly done in `steps.md`.

**2. Phase 2 (settings and model connections): done.**
- **Switchable connections:** Ollama chat, Ollama embeddings and Claude each sit behind a common interface. A registry picks them from the settings and rejects any provider that is disabled or unknown.
- **Usage and cost data:** every answer streams back and ends with token counts and the reason it stopped. The future stats and costs tabs need this.
- **Tests:** 34 offline tests pass, plus 2 tests against the real Ollama. The real embeddings ranked a backup question closest to the backup text, as expected.
- **Bug fixed:** the tests caught one bug. Claude's "overloaded" error (HTTP 529) was being reported as a permanent failure instead of a retryable one.

**Changes to the Claude settings, based on Anthropic's current API guidance:**
- The default model is now **`claude-opus-5`** instead of `claude-sonnet-5`. Change `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` if you'd rather use Sonnet, which is cheaper.
- Temperature isn't sent, because Opus 5 and Sonnet 5 no longer accept it.
- I've **turned on Anthropic's server-side refusal fallback**: if Opus 5's safety filters decline a request, it's re-run on another model. Switch it off with `ANTHROPIC_REFUSAL_FALLBACK=false`.
- The system prompt is cached, which lowers the cost of repeated questions.

**3. ⚠️ Local generation is too slow on this machine.** It produces about 2.5 tokens per second and reads the prompt at 4–13 tokens per second. The cause is that the VM's virtual CPU ("QEMU Virtual CPU") hides the host's AVX instructions, so Ollama falls back to slow code. A normal RAG prompt would take several minutes per answer. Embeddings are fast enough, at about 0.1 s per question. How do you want to handle it?
- **a)** Change the VM's CPU type to "host" (in Proxmox/libvirt/QEMU) so the real CPU instructions are visible. I'd expect this to be 5–10× faster, and it's the best fix if you can do it.
- **b)** Switch to a smaller model (`gemma3:1b`) and put fewer chunks in the prompt. This is faster but gives weaker answers.
- **c)** Use Claude for the answers, which needs your `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in `.env`, and keep Ollama only for embeddings.

This choice doesn't block Phase 3 (document loading, chunking, the vector store and tuning `MIN_SCORE`), because Phase 3 only needs embeddings. It does matter for Phase 4. I've recorded all of this in `research.md`.

Nothing has been pushed yet. Should I start Phase 3?

### 🤖 Claude

I pushed Phase 2 to `origin/dev`. Phase 3 is finished and committed locally but not pushed yet, so tell me if you want it pushed too. Retrieval passed with a large margin: using the real `embeddinggemma` embeddings, the expected documents came back for almost every test question.

**What was built:**
- **Chunking:** the articles are split by heading, and long sections are packed into windows of about 400 tokens with overlap. The 5 articles give 49 chunks, and every section fits in one. Each chunk keeps its heading path (e.g. `Configuring SAML 2.0 › Certificate rotation`) for the citations.
- **Vector store:** an exact in-memory search behind a common interface, so pgvector could replace it later.
- **Index cache on disk:** it's rebuilt automatically when a document, the chunking settings or the embedding model change. It's written so that a crash can't leave a broken index. Loading it takes 6 ms, compared with 5 minutes to build it on this machine.
- **Retrieval benchmark:** a reusable module that the future hidden Tests tab can call. Run it with `uv run python -m app.evaluation.retrieval` or `pytest -m eval`.
- **Tests:** 58 offline tests and 4 benchmark tests pass; lint and type checks are clean.

**Results with the real embeddings (top 5 results per question):**

| Metric | Result | Target |
|---|---|---|
| Share of expected documents found | **0.97** | ≥ 0.8 |
| Questions with at least one expected document found | **1.00** | — |
| Off-topic questions rejected by `MIN_SCORE=0.35` | 3 of 3 (scores 0.03–0.18) | — |
| Weakest answerable question | 0.416 | stays above 0.35 |

**Two findings that matter for Phase 4:**
1. **The similarity threshold can't catch questions that are near a topic but not answered by the docs.** LDAP (0.52) and mobile offline mode (0.45) score as high as real answers. The threshold still rejects clearly off-topic questions cheaply, but for these cases the answer prompt must tell the model to refuse when the retrieved text doesn't contain the answer.
2. **Correction:** earlier I said embeddings take about 0.1 s here. That was wrong. It's about 0.7–1 s per question and about 6 s per chunk when building the index. I've corrected `research.md`.

**The decision for Phase 4 is still open.** Local answers run at about 2.5 tokens/s, so a full answer would take minutes. The options are the same as before:
- **a)** change the VM's CPU type to "host"
- **b)** use a smaller model
- **c)** use Claude for the answers, which needs your `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in `.env`

The Phase 4 code works with any of them. To test it end to end, though, I need either (a) or an API key for (c), or I'll run with a much smaller local model. Which do you prefer?

---

  "content": "\"\"\"Prompt templates live in app/prompts/*.md so they can be reviewed and versioned like code\n(and later gain variants, e.g. a \"detailed\" answer style, ideas.md #3).\"\"\"\n\nfrom collections.abc import Sequence\nfrom functools import lru_cache\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom app.stores.vector.base import SearchResult\n\nPROMPTS_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / \"prompts\"\n\n\n@lru_cache\ndef load_prompt(name: str) -> str:\n    return (PROMPTS_DIR / f\"{name}.md\").read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\").strip()\n\n\ndef build_user_message(question: str, sources: Sequence[SearchResult]) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Numbered sources first, then the question. Numbers are 1-based positions in `sources`.\"\"\"\n    blocks = []\n    for number, result in enumerate(sources, start=1):\n        chunk = result.chunk\n        header = f\"Document: {chunk.title}\"\n        if chunk.section:\n            header += f\"\\nSection: {chunk.section}\"\n        blocks.append(f'<source id=\"{number}\">\\n{header}\\n\\n{chunk.text}\\n</source>')\n    return \"<sources>\\n\" + \"\\n\".join(blocks) + f\"\\n</sources>\\n\\nQuestion: {question.strip()}\"\n"
}

### 🤖 Claude

I've built and committed the Phase 4 code (`5bcefda`), but I can't run the evaluation with Claude yet: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in `.env` is still empty. That end-to-end run is the phase's "done when" check, so I've marked it 🔍 in `steps.md` rather than done.

**To continue, edit `.env` yourself** (please don't paste the key into the chat):
```
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-ant-...
LLM_PROVIDER=anthropic
```
Then tell me, and I'll run all 18 questions against Claude: `uv run python -m app.evaluation.answers --show`. With `claude-opus-5` that should cost well under $1.

**What Phase 4 does:**
- **How a question is answered:** the pipeline finds the relevant chunks and drops any below `MIN_SCORE`. If nothing relevant remains, it replies with a polite "not in the knowledge base, ask an expert" message **without calling the model**. Otherwise the model gets the numbered sources and streams its answer, and the `[n]` markers become citations.
- **Prompts** live in `app/prompts/system.md` and `no_answer.md`. The rules: answer only from the sources, cite every fact, refuse without citations when the answer isn't there, stay professional and concise, reply in the language of the question, and ignore any instructions inside the sources.
- **How refusals are detected:** an answer with no citations counts as a refusal. So does a safety-filter decline from Claude. This works whatever language the reply is in.
- **Per-turn data:** every turn returns a full result with token usage, timings, the relevance score and the refusal reason, and a hook records it once. For now the hook only writes metrics to the log, not the question text.
- **Tests:** 75 offline tests pass; lint and type checks are clean.

**What the local Ollama run showed:** a single question **timed out after 5 minutes**, because this CPU couldn't read the roughly 1,500-token prompt within the backend's 300-second read timeout. I fixed two things:
- the error now says it timed out, instead of the misleading "Ollama is unreachable"
- the timeout is configurable with `OLLAMA_TIMEOUT_S`

This confirms that local answers aren't usable until you change the VM's CPU type. I've saved a memory note so I re-run the Ollama measurements after that change.

Phase 3 and 4 are committed locally only, not pushed. Should I push them along with the evaluation results?

### 🤖 Claude

Phase 5 (the backend API) is finished and verified end to end in Docker through nginx. It's committed as `ff3fcf2` but not pushed yet. Earlier phases are pushed.

**Endpoints** (Swagger documentation at http://localhost:8080/api/v1/... and `/docs` on the backend):

| Endpoint | What it does |
|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/health` | Always returns 200, with status `starting` / `ok` / `degraded`, whether the index is ready, and the Ollama status |
| `GET /api/v1/providers` | Lists the enabled models and whether each is usable. Without a key, Claude is shown as unavailable with the reason |
| `POST /api/v1/chat` | `{message, conversation_id?, options: {provider?}}` → answer, citations, whether it refused and why, token usage, timings |
| `POST /api/v1/chat/stream` | The same answer as a stream of events: `meta` (ids and candidate sources) → `token`… → `done` (full result), or `error` |

**How it behaves:**
- **Errors:** every error has the same shape, `{"error": {"code", "message"}}`. A bad request returns 422, an unknown or disabled provider 400, an index that's still loading or a provider that's down 503, and a provider error 502.
- **Unsupported options:** anything other than `provider` inside `options` (for example `language`) is rejected with 422 rather than silently ignored. Language and detail options can be added later without breaking existing clients.
- **Startup:** the index loads in the background, so the API answers immediately with `starting` / 503 until it's ready.
- **Logs:** one JSON line per request, tagged with a request id. The chat metrics carry the same id.
- **Tests:** 95 offline tests pass, 20 of them new API tests.

**Verified in Docker:**
- The index was built on the volume (~5 minutes on this CPU), and after a restart it loaded from the cache within seconds.
- Claude answered through `/chat` in 5.3 s with 3 citations.
- A German question through `/chat/stream` was answered in German, with the first token after 2.3 s.

**Two real bugs found and fixed along the way:**
1. **The index couldn't be saved in Docker.** It was built in a temporary folder next to `data/index` and then renamed into place. In Docker, `data/index` is a mounted volume, so the rename crossed filesystems and failed, wasting the 5-minute build. The build now happens inside the index folder. The unit tests couldn't catch this because they run on a single filesystem; a new test now checks where the files are moved.
2. **A configuration error at startup hung the API.** An unknown provider or a missing KB folder was retried forever, leaving the API stuck on "loading". Now only temporary errors (such as Ollama still starting) are retried, and configuration errors are reported in `/health` straight away.

**Also worth knowing:**
- Claude's answers include some extra detail. For example, the rate-limit question also got burst limits and the API Boost add-on. That's within the prompt's rules, and "more detail only on request" is idea #3 in `ideas.md`, so I've left it for now.
- Your `.env` still has `TOP_K=5`, which overrides the new default of 6.

Should I push Phase 5 and move on to **Phase 6 (the chat frontend)**?

  "content": "import type { StreamEvent } from \"./types\";\n\n/**\n * Incremental Server-Sent Events parser. Network chunks can split an event anywhere,\n * so text is buffered until a blank line ends the event.\n * (EventSource can't be used: it only supports GET, and the chat stream is a POST.)\n */\nexport class SseParser {\n  private buffer = \"\";\n\n  push(chunk: string): StreamEvent[] {\n    this.buffer += chunk.replace(/\\r\\n/g, \"\\n\");\n    const events: StreamEvent[] = [];\n    let end: number;\n    while ((end = this.buffer.indexOf(\"\\n\\n\")) !== -1) {\n      const block = this.buffer.slice(0, end);\n      this.buffer = this.buffer.slice(end + 2);\n      const event = parseBlock(block);\n      if (event) events.push(event);\n    }\n    return events;\n  }\n}\n\nfunction parseBlock(block: string): StreamEvent | null {\n  let name = \"message\";\n  const data: string[] = [];\n  for (const line of block.split(\"\\n\")) {\n    if (line.startsWith(\":\")) continue; // comment / keep-alive\n    const colon = line.indexOf(\":\");\n    const field = colon === -1 ? line : line.slice(0, colon);\n    const value = colon === -1 ? \"\" : line.slice(colon + 1).replace(/^ /, \"\");\n    if (field === \"event\") name = value;\n    else if (field === \"data\") data.push(value);\n  }\n  if (data.length === 0) return null;\n  try {\n    return { event: name, data: JSON.parse(data.join(\"\\n\")) } as St

{
  "file_path": "/home/sako/git/ep_kb_rag_chatbot/frontend/src/features/chat/citations.ts",
  "content": "// Same marker syntax the backend parses (app/rag/citations.py): [1], [2, 3], [1][3].\nconst MARKER = /\\[(\\d+(?:\\s*,\\s*\\d+)*)\\]/g;\n\nexport const citationAnchor = (messageId: string, n: number) => `cite-${messageId}-${n}`;\n\n/**\n * Turns [n] markers into Markdown links (#cite-<message>-<n>) so the renderer can show them\n * as clickable chips. Numbers without a matching source are left as plain text.\n */\nexport function linkCitations(answer: string, messageId: string, known: Set<number>): string {\n  return answer.replace(MARKER, (whole, group: string) => {\n    const numbers = group.split(\",\").map((n) => Number(n.trim()));\n    if (!numbers.every((n) => known.has(n))) return whole;\n    return numbers.map((n) => `[${n}](#${citationAnchor(messageId, n)})`).join(\"\");\n  });\n}\n"
}

### 🤖 Claude

The conversation is now saved to Markdown automatically, and Phase 6 (the chat UI) is finished and committed. My previous reply was cut off partway, so here is the full summary. Three commits are local only: Phase 5 (`ff3fcf2`), the logging (`fa38a3c`) and Phase 6 (`3c51813`).

**1. Conversation logs (`fa38a3c`)**
- `scripts/export_ai_logs.py` turns Claude Code's session transcripts into readable Markdown in `documentation/ai-logs/`:
  - your prompts and my replies in full
  - tool calls folded away and shortened
  - internal reasoning left out
  - API keys and tokens replaced with `[REDACTED]`
- Two hooks in `.claude/settings.json` run it automatically: after every reply I finish, and when a session ends.
- Both sessions so far are exported, including the earlier one that created `CLAUDE.md`, and there's an index in `documentation/ai-logs/README.md`.
- I checked the files: your real API key doesn't appear in them.
- ⚠️ **One action for you:** the `.claude/` folder didn't exist when this session started, so Claude Code may not have loaded the hooks yet. Open `/hooks` once, or restart Claude Code, to activate them. Until then I re-export the logs by hand before every commit, and I've saved that as a standing rule.

**2. Phase 6: the chat UI (`3c51813`)**
- **Chat:**
  - answers stream in word by word and render formatting such as bold, lists and code
  - each `[n]` becomes a small numbered chip; clicking it highlights and opens the matching source card
  - each source card shows the document, section, a snippet and a match score
- **States:** a refusal is shown in amber ("Not covered by the knowledge base"). There are also clear states for "searching", "writing the answer", errors with **Try again**, and "knowledge base loading". A status indicator in the header shows whether the knowledge base is ready.
- **Controls:**
  - a **Model** selector listing the available providers, which lets you use Claude while Ollama is slow
  - **New conversation**
  - example questions to click
  - an empty `/admin` page reserved for the future hidden tabs
- **Checks:** 14 frontend tests, eslint and the type check are clean. All 96 backend tests pass.
- **Browser test:** I ran a real headless browser against the Docker stack:
  - an English answer with 8 citation chips
  - an off-topic question refused in 0.7 s
  - a German question answered in German
  - "New conversation" and `/admin` both worked
  - no console errors
- **Snippet fix:** the source snippets showed raw Markdown (`**`, backticks, table pipes). They now come back from the API as plain text.

**Worth knowing:** Claude's answers tend to add "additional details" beyond the direct answer. That's within the current prompt rules, and "more detail only on request" is idea #3 in `ideas.md`.

Should I push the three commits to `origin/dev`? Once you've checked the UI at http://localhost:8080, Phase 7 is next: tidying Docker Compose and a clean check from a fresh clone.

### 🤖 Claude

Phase 7 is finished: a fresh clone of the repo comes up and works end to end in the browser. It's committed locally (`b43f437`, `03c64d4`) but not pushed yet. Phases 5, 6 and the logging are already on `origin/dev`.

**Fresh-clone check.** I cloned the `dev` branch, created `.env` from `.env.example` with only your API key added, and started it with empty volumes:

| Step | Result |
|---|---|
| `docker compose up -d --build` | Finished after **9.5 minutes**, almost all of it downloading the ~4 GB of models |
| While the index was building | `/health` said `starting` and `/chat` returned 503 `not_ready` |
| Index built | **About 5 minutes later** (5 documents, 49 chunks) |
| Browser test | Cited answer, off-topic refusal, German answer, "New conversation" and `/admin` all passed, with **no console errors** |

After the test I removed the test stack with its volumes (the 4 GB model copy) and deleted the clone, which held a copy of your key. Your main stack is running again, using its cached index.

**What changed in this phase:**
- **Pinned images:** `ollama/ollama:0.34.4`, `uv:0.12`, `nginx:stable-alpine`, instead of `latest`.
- **Ollama always runs.** The optional profile was a trap: without `COMPOSE_PROFILES` in `.env`, Ollama didn't start, and the backend couldn't build its index because embeddings always need Ollama. Now plain `docker compose up` works even without a `.env`.
- **Health and port:** a frontend healthcheck, and the web port can be set with `FRONTEND_PORT` (default 8080).
- **nginx:** security headers including a Content Security Policy, gzip (the JS bundle drops from 386 KB to 139 KB), year-long caching for the hashed build files, and the request id passed through to the backend.
- **Bug fixed:** after a rebuild, compose had kept the backend on its old image. I forced a recreate, and it loaded the cached index in seconds.

**Two small things in your `.env`** (I don't edit it, because it contains your key):
- delete `COMPOSE_PROFILES=ollama`; it's no longer used
- set `TOP_K=6` if you haven't already

The remaining phases are **8** (tests and the speed/quality baseline, plus the optional CI) and **9** (the README and final log export). Should I push Phase 7 and continue with Phase 8? And do you want the GitHub Actions CI workflow in Phase 8?

### 🤖 Claude

Phase 8 (tests, evaluation baseline and performance tests) is done and committed as `2ba0524`. It isn't pushed yet. My previous reply was cut off before the commit ran; nothing was lost, so I re-ran the checks and committed.

**Results (details in `documentation/evaluation.md`):**

| Area | Result |
|---|---|
| Backend unit and API tests | 101 pass, 95% of the code covered |
| Frontend tests | 14 pass |
| Retrieval (real embeddings, top 6) | Finds 97% of the expected documents; at least one expected document for every question |
| Answers with Claude (`claude-opus-5`) | **18 / 18**: 15 answered correctly with every key fact, 3 correctly refused, the German question answered in German |
| Answer speed with Claude | First words after 2.6 s (p50) / 9.9 s (p95); full answer 4.8 s / 11.5 s |
| Cost | About **$0.011 per question**, about 38% of input tokens from the prompt cache; one full benchmark run costs about $0.20 |
| Speed without the model | Vector search over 50,000 chunks: p95 15 ms. API: 450 requests/s, p95 54 ms |

**What was added:**
- **Evaluation output:** the evaluation tools now report p50 and p95 latencies, and `--json` saves a summary. The baseline files are in `documentation/eval/`, and the future hidden Tests tab can reuse the same code.
- **Performance tests:** 5 offline tests under a new `perf` marker. Their thresholds are loose enough to pass on a slow CI machine.
- **Coverage:** `uv run pytest --cov` reports test coverage.
- **CI:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. Every push will run the backend checks, the frontend checks and a Docker image build on GitHub. It uses no secrets and doesn't need Ollama or Claude.
- **Docs:** the README checklist item "Unit, speed, and performance test" is ticked.

**Local model:** local answers with Ollama still aren't measured, because this VM's CPU lacks AVX. `evaluation.md` says so, and I'll re-run the numbers after you switch the VM's CPU type to "host".

Should I push? That also runs the new CI on GitHub for the first time. The `gh` CLI isn't logged in here, so you'll see the result under the Actions tab. After that comes the last phase: the **README** (architecture, API, trade-offs, how to run and test, AI tool usage) and the final log export.

Images are pinned, with healthchecks, security headers and a CSP.\n- **Knowledge base:** 5 mock OmniCorp articles, plus 18 evaluation questions (including cross-document, German, and questions the docs can't answer).\n- **AI conversation logs** (mandatory deliverable): exported automatically to `documentation/ai-logs/`, with secrets redacted.\n\n## Results (see `documentation/evaluation.md`)\n\

### 🤖 Claude

I've checked everything we built. The code, tests, Docker stack and CI all work. The main gap is the **README (Phase 9)**, which the brief requires and which isn't written yet. I found and fixed one real bug and some outdated docs (committed as `eff0390`, not pushed yet).

**Checked and working:**
- **Tests:** everything passes locally — 101 backend, 5 performance, 6 against live Ollama and the retrieval benchmark, and 14 frontend. Lint, type checks and the frontend build are clean.
- **CI:** green on GitHub for the latest pushed commit.
- **Running stack:** all services healthy, index loaded (49 chunks), both models present, and both providers available.
- **Fresh clone:** the Phase 7 check from an empty setup through to a working browser chat still stands.
- **Secrets:** your API key isn't in any tracked file or anywhere in git history, and `.env` isn't tracked.
- **Conversation logs:** the hook now updates them automatically after each of my replies, including your latest message.
- **Config:** `.env.example` matches the settings the code reads (the two differences are intentional).

**Fixed during this review:**
1. **nginx timeout bug.** nginx waited at most 300 s for the backend, exactly the backend's own Ollama timeout. With a slow model, nginx could cut the connection first, so the UI would say "The answer was interrupted" instead of the clear "Ollama did not respond within 300 s" message. nginx now waits up to 900 s. I checked the logic but didn't reproduce it, because that takes a 5-minute Ollama run.
2. **Outdated docs.** Four statements that later decisions overrode are corrected: the removed Ollama profile, the old default Claude model, the old index file names, and the old name of the provider setting.

**Still missing or needing your decision:**

| # | Item | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **README (Phase 9)** | The brief requires architecture decisions, API design, trade-offs, how to run and test, and a note on which AI tools were used for what. None of that is written yet. The README still contains only the assignment brief and checklist. |
| 2 | **`LLM_PROVIDER=ollama` in your `.env`** | The UI starts on Ollama, so on this VM a first question waits 5 minutes and then fails. For demos, set `LLM_PROVIDER=anthropic` until you change the VM's CPU type. |
| 3 | **Follow-up questions don't use earlier context** | The chat keeps a conversation id, but each question is answered on its own, so "and on the Enterprise plan?" loses the earlier topic. It fits the plan (history is idea #5), but a reviewer may try it. I can add it, or document it as a known limitation. |
| 4 | **No authentication or rate limiting on the API** | Fine for a local prototype, but with a Claude key anyone on the network could run up costs. I'll document it as a trade-off. |
| 5 | **Local Ollama answers never measured** | Blocked by the VM's CPU; noted in `evaluation.md` and in memory for after your VM change. |
| 6 | **Pull request to `main` not created** | `gh` still needs `! gh auth login`, or you can open https://github.com/sarkiszhamakortsyan/ep_kb_rag_chatbot/compare/main...dev yourself. |

Some checklist items in the README are already partly done:
- **Polite refusal with a pointer to a human expert:** implemented, both for the low-score cutoff and in the prompt.
- **Answering in the question's language:** works, as the German test question showed.
- **Professional, concise tone:** enforced by the prompt.

The README checklist doesn't show these yet. I'll mark them as partial in Phase 9.

**Suggested order:**
1. Push `eff0390`.
2. Do Phase 9 (the README) on `dev`.
3. Decide on item 3.
4. Open the pull request, which will then include the README.

Should I go ahead?

### 🤖 Claude

I opened **PR #2 to `main`**: https://github.com/sarkiszhamakortsyan/ep_kb_rag_chatbot/pull/2. All CI checks pass (backend, frontend and Docker, on both the push and the PR runs). I haven't merged it; that's your call.

`dev` already had a merged pull request (#1, "Dev", merged at 20:14 UTC), which brought everything up to Phase 8 into `main`. So PR #2 only contains the two newer commits: the review fixes and Phase 9.

What got done, in the order I suggested:

1. **Pushed the review fixes** (`eff0390`): the nginx timeout and the stale docs.
2. **Re-measured the local model on the host CPU.** The VM now has an Intel i5-8300H with AVX2.

   | | Before (emulated CPU) | Now |
   |---|---|---|
   | Embedding a question | ~1 s | **~50 ms** |
   | Building the index | ~5 min | **16 s** |
   | Off-topic refusal | 0.8 s | **~50 ms** |
   | Local answer (`gemma3:4b`) | timed out | **~80 s** (p50) |

   On the 18-question evaluation set, the local model passes **14/18**, against 18/18 for Claude:
   - Three of the misses are correct answers that leave out one detail.
   - The fourth is an **unsupported claim**: it said OmniCorp "does not integrate with LDAP", which the documentation doesn't say. Claude correctly refused that question.
   - That's why the README recommends Claude for quality and speed, and the local model for privacy or offline use.
3. **Wrote the README** (Phase 9, `f71e607`). It covers everything the brief asks for:
   - quick start
   - architecture, with a Mermaid diagram
   - the API, with request/response examples and error codes
   - decisions and trade-offs
   - testing and results (Claude and the local model side by side)
   - configuration and project structure
   - known limitations
   - which AI tools were used for which parts

   The original brief and the checklist stay at the end, with the checklist updated. I checked that every link and anchor resolves.
4. **Follow-up questions:** they're documented as a known limitation rather than built, because multi-turn context belongs to the response-history idea (#5), and the project rules say ideas are built only when you ask.
5. **Opened the pull request.** The secret scan was clean before pushing, and the AI logs are exported and committed.

**Two things for you:**
- **Please review the "AI assistant usage" section of the README.** It's written in your voice ("Me (the developer)": goals, approvals, decisions, reviews), so check that it describes your role the way you want before you submit.
- **Your `.env` still has `LLM_PROVIDER=ollama`.** Local answers work now, but each takes about 80 s, so for a demo I'd set `LLM_PROVIDER=anthropic`, or pick *anthropic* in the UI's Model selector.

305:## AI assistant usage
## AI assistant usage

The assignment asks which AI tools were used, and for which parts. The complete conversation logs are in **[`documentation/ai-logs/`](documentation/ai-logs/)**. They were exported automatically after every assistant turn by a Claude Code hook (`.claude/settings.json` → `scripts/export_ai_logs.py`, with secrets redacted).

| Tool | Used for |
|---|---|
| **Claude Code** (CLI, Claude Opus 5.5 model) | Everything in this repository was built in Claude Code sessions, directed and reviewed by me phase by phase (see the prompts in the logs): the research and decision documents in `documentation/taskdocs/`; the mock knowledge-base articles and evaluation set; all backend and frontend code and tests; the Docker and nginx setup; CI; the evaluation runs; this README; and the log exporter |
| **Me (the developer)** | The goal, requirements and task documents; approving the plan and each phase; decisions (Claude vs local model, branch and commit workflow, VM CPU change); reviewing results |
| **Claude API** (`claude-opus-5`) at runtime | Answer generation when the *anthropic* provider is selected. Part of the product, not a development tool |

Also used during development: a throwaway headless Chromium (Playwright) for browser end-to-end checks, which isn't part of the project dependencies.

### 🤖 Claude

All five benchmarks are done. Two models beat the current `gemma3:4b`: **`qwen3.5:4b`** gives the best answers, and **`ministral-3:3b`** gives slightly better answers than `gemma3:4b` while also being faster.

I ran the same 18-question set on each model with the same retrieval, prompt and CPU:

| Model | Passed | Answerable (15) | Unanswerable refused (3) | Answer time p50 / p95 | Download |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`qwen3.5:4b`** (thinking off) | **16 / 18** | 13 | 3 | 119 s / 172 s | 3.4 GB |
| **`ministral-3:3b`** | **15 / 18** | 12 | 3 | **63 s** / 92 s | 3.0 GB |
| `gemma3:4b` (current) | 14 / 18 | 12 | **2** (the LDAP claim) | 79 s / 101 s | 3.3 GB |
| `llama3.2:3b` | 12 / 18 | 9 | 3 | 48 s / 68 s | 2.0 GB |
| `granite4.2:3b` | 10 / 18 | 7 | 3 | 61 s / 77 s | 2.2 GB |
| `phi4-mini` 3.8B | 9 / 18 | 6 | 3 | 66 s / 85 s | 2.5 GB |

- **`qwen3.5:4b`:** its two misses (q11, q13) each leave out one of two key facts. It is about 50% slower than `gemma3:4b`, around 2 minutes per answer.
- **`ministral-3:3b`:** it refused all 3 unanswerable questions, and each of its 3 misses (q06, q10, q11) is an answer that leaves out a detail.
  - It has an Apache-2.0 license.
  - It answered the German question in German, and so did `qwen3.5:4b`.
- **`gemma3:4b`** is the only model that made an unsupported claim (the LDAP answer).
- **The other three** refused correctly but missed too many facts to be useful.

**My recommendation:** make `ministral-3:3b` the default local model. At about 1 minute per answer it is the most usable of the stronger models on this CPU. I'd document `qwen3.5:4b` as the option when answer quality matters more than speed. It needs `OLLAMA_THINK=false`, otherwise it spends minutes reasoning first. Claude stays the recommendation for demos (18/18, about 5 s).

The benchmark results are in the scratchpad for now; nothing in the repo has changed yet. Should I switch the default to `ministral-3:3b`, or keep `gemma3:4b`?

Switching means updating:
- `config.py`, `docker-compose.yml` and `.env.example`
- `evaluation.md`, `research.md` and the README
- the result files in `documentation/eval/`

Then I'd rebuild, check the stack runs, and push to PR #2. Your `.env` sets `OLLAMA_CHAT_MODEL=gemma3:4b`, so you'd need to change that line yourself.

Sources:
- [Best CPU-only local LLMs in 2026](https://www.popularai.org/p/best-cpu-only-local-llm-2026)
- [Best Ollama Models 2026 (Morph)](https://www.morphllm.com/best-ollama-models)
- [Small LLMs That Fit in 8GB (Pinggy)](https://pinggy.io/blog/small_llms_that_fit_in_8gb_memory/)
- [Ollama model library](https://ollama.com/search?o=newest)

# Embedding provider (Anthropic has no embeddings API): ollama
EMBEDDING_PROVIDER=ollama

# --- Ollama ----------------------------------------------------------------
OLLAMA_BASE_URL=http://localhost:11434
OLLAMA_CHAT_MODEL=ministral-3:3b
OLLAMA_EMBED_MODEL=embeddinggemma
# Context window; must fit the system prompt + retrieved chunks + question
OLLAMA_NUM_CTX=4096
OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE=30m
# Leave empty for models without a thinking mode; set false for qwen3 etc.
OLLAMA_THINK=
# Max seconds to wait for the first token (a RAG prompt on a slow CPU can take minutes).
# Keep it below nginx's proxy_read_timeout (900 s, frontend/nginx.conf).
OLLAMA_TIMEOUT_S=300
```

<summary>Tool result</summary>

```
On CPU, chat workloads are limited to about 3–4B-parameter models. Anything larger is too slow for interactive chat. The candidates are:

| Model (Ollama tag) | Size | Why / why not |
|---|---|---|
| **`gemma3:4b`** ✅ default | ~3.3 GB | Strong instruction following for its size, 128K context, 140+ languages (covers the multi-language feature), no "thinking" tokens to wait for on CPU |
| `qwen3:4b` (alternative) | ~2.6 GB | Very good reasoning and multilingual support. It has a thinking mode, which **must be disabled** (`think=false`), or CPU latency explodes. Good second choice to benchmark |
| `llama3.2:3b` | ~2.0 GB | Fastest and very popular, but officially supports only 8 languages and follows grounding/citation rules less reliably |
| `phi4-mini` (3.8B) | ~2.5 GB | Good reasoning, weaker multilingual |
| `llama3.1:8b` / larger | 5 GB+ | Better answers, but too slow on 4 CPU cores. Worth considering only if the reviewer has a GPU (the tag is configurable) |

**Embedding model**: **`embeddinggemma`** (300M params, 768-dim, 100+ languages, fast on CPU). It matches the multi-language requirement. The fallback is `nomic-embed-text` (the most popular English default), and `qwen3-embedding:0.6b` / `bge-m3` if retrieval quality needs a boost. Note that embeddinggemma expects task prefixes (`task: search result | query: …` for queries, `title: … | text: …` for documents).
| Area | Decision |
|---|---|
| Backend | Python 3.12, FastAPI, Pydantic, raw `ollama` + `anthropic` SDKs, p

```
- **Backend:** Python 3.12 + FastAPI. Heading-aware chunking, exact in-memory vector search with an on-disk index cache, and a streaming RAG pipeline with citation mapping.
- **Frontend:** Vite + React + TypeScript + Tailwind. Streamed answers, clickable `[n]` citation chips and source cards.
- **LLM:** a local model via **Ollama** (`gemma3:4b`, embeddings `embeddinggemma`), or **Claude** with your own API key (`claude-opus-5`), selectable per question.
- **One command:** `docker compose up`.

| **Heading-aware chunking** | Sections stay coherent, and the heading path gives human-readable citations | Tuned for structured Markdown articles, not arbitrary PDFs |
| **Similarity threshold before the LLM** | Off-topic questions are refused in about 50 ms at zero token cost | Can't separate *near-topic* unanswerable questions (they score like real ones), so the prompt handles those; calibration data is in `storage.md` |
| **"No valid citation = refusal"** | Language-independent refusal detection, no second LLM call | Depends on the model following the citation rule; measured at 100% on the eval set |
| **Provider registries (LLM and embeddings separate)** | Switch models by configuration or per request; fakes in tests; Anthropic has no embeddings API, so embeddings are their own interface | More indirection than hard-coding one model |
| **`claude-opus-5` default for Claude, server-side refusal fallback, cached system prompt** | Anthropic's current recommended default; a safety-fil
… [4122 more characters omitted]
```

```
async def _events(request: ChatRequest, services: Services) -> AsyncIterator[str]:
    try:
        async for event in services.pipeline.stream(
            request.message,
            conversation_id=request.conversation_id,
            options=ChatOptions(provider=request.options.provider),
        ):
            if isinstance(event, SourcesEvent):
                yield _sse(
                    "meta",
                    {
                        "conversation_id": event.conversation_id,
                        "message_id": event.message_id,
                        "sources": [
                            CitationOut.from_citation(s).model_dump() for s in event.sources
                        ],
                    },
                )
            elif isinstance(event, AnswerDelta):
                yield _sse("token", {"text": event.text})
            elif isinstance(event, Completed):
                yield _sse("done", ChatResponse.from_result(event.result).model_dump())
    except Exception as exc:
        # Headers are already sent, so errors travel as an SSE event.
        _, code, message = error_payload(exc)
        if code == "internal_error":
            logger.exception("Streaming chat failed")
        else:
            logger.warning("Streaming chat failed: %s", exc)
        yield _sse("error", {"code": code, "message": message})


def _sse(event: str, data: object) -> str:
    return f"event: {event}\ndata: {json.dumps(data, ensure_ascii=False)}\n\n"
```

### 🤖 Claude

`ministral-3:3b` fits the task well, so I made it the default local model. It's pushed to `dev` as `71af19f`, and CI on PR #2 is green.

**The test:**
- **Second benchmark run:** 16/18, against 15/18 in the first run. It refused all 3 unanswerable questions both times.
  - Median answer time was about 61 s, against 79 s for `gemma3:4b`.
  - One answer took 198 s even though it produced the same number of tokens as in run 1, so the VM was probably busy at that moment.
- **Its misses:**
  - **q06:** a correct answer worded differently from the phrase the benchmark checks for.
  - **q11:** leaves out one detail.
  - **q10, first run only:** picked the 10% credit tier instead of 25%; the second run got it right.
  - It never made an unsupported claim like `gemma3:4b`'s LDAP answer.
- **Through the Docker stack (browser → nginx → backend → model):**
  - English question: streamed with citations in 21 s.
  - German question: answered in German in 46 s.
  - Pricing question: refused, with no citations.

**What changed:**
- The default model in `config.py`, `docker-compose.yml` and `.env.example`, plus a comment there naming `qwen3.5:4b` as the slower, higher-quality option.
- The model comparison table in `evaluation.md`, a decision addendum in `research.md`, and updated results in the README.
- The raw results of all six models in `documentation/eval/ollama-models/`.
- A short note in `steps.md` and `CLAUDE.md`.
- The conversation log.

**For you:**
- **Change your `.env`:** it still sets `OLLAMA_CHAT_MODEL=gemma3:4b`, so your stack uses the old model until you change it. I ran the end-to-end test with a temporary override and then restored your settings. After editing, run `docker compose up -d backend`; the model is already downloaded.
- **Two `/export` text files** are still untracked in the repo root: `2026-09-25-211020-….txt` and `2026-09-26-163714-….txt`. Should I move them into `documentation/ai-logs/` after a secret check, or delete them?
- **Free about 10 GB of disk:** the other four candidate models are still in the Ollama volume. `gemma3:4b` isn't the default any more either, so it can go too.

from collections.abc import Sequence

import httpx

from app.providers.embeddings.base import EmbeddingDocument, EmbeddingProvider, Vector
from app.providers.errors import ProviderError, ProviderUnavailableError

_TIMEOUT = httpx.Timeout(connect=5.0, read=300.0, write=30.0, pool=5.0)

# Retrieval models are trained with task prefixes; omitting them hurts ranking.
# Keys are matched against the start of the model name. Documents use {title} and {text}.
_PROMPTS: dict[str, tuple[str, str]] = {
    "embeddinggemma": ("task: search result | query: {text}", "title: {title} | text: {text}"),
    "nomic-embed-text": ("search_query: {text}", "search_document: {text}"),
}
_PLAIN = ("{text}", "{text}")


class OllamaEmbeddingProvider(EmbeddingProvider):
    """Embeds text with Ollama's batch endpoint `/api/embed`."""

    name = "ollama"

    def __init__(
        self,
        base_url: str,
        model: str,
        *,
        batch_size: int = 16,
        keep_alive: str = "30m",
        client: httpx.AsyncClient | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        self.model = model
        self._batch_size = batch_size
        self._keep_alive = keep_alive
        self._query_prompt, self._document_prompt = next(
            (p for prefix, p in _PROMPTS.items() if model.startswith(prefix)), _PLAIN
        )
        self._client = client or httpx.AsyncClient(base_url=base_url, timeout=_TIMEOUT)

```
async def evaluate_retrieval(
    retriever: Retriever, questions: list[EvalQuestion], k: int
) -> RetrievalReport:
    results = []
    for q in questions:
        retrieval = await retriever.retrieve(q.question, top_k=k)
        retrieved = list(dict.fromkeys(r.chunk.doc_id for r in retrieval.results))
        found = [d for d in q.expected_doc_ids if d in retrieved]
        results.append(
            QuestionResult(
                id=q.id,
                answerable=q.answerable,
                expected=q.expected_doc_ids,
                retrieved=retrieved,
                top_score=retrieval.top_score,
                recall=len(found) / len(q.expected_doc_ids) if q.expected_doc_ids else 1.0,
                latency_ms=retrieval.embed_ms + retrieval.search_ms,
            )
        )
    return RetrievalReport(k=k, results=results)


class QuestionResult:
    id: str
    answerable: bool
    expected: list[str]
    retrieved: list[str]  # distinct doc ids in rank order
    top_score: float
    recall: float  # share of expected docs found in the top k (1.0 for unanswerable)
    latency_ms: float


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RetrievalReport:
    k: int
    results: list[QuestionResult]

    @property
```

"""Embedding provider interface, kept separate from chat providers.

Anthropic has no embeddings API, so "Claude only" (ideas.md #7) needs a non-Ollama
implementation of this interface (in-process model or Voyage AI) rather than a new pipeline.
"""

from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
from collections.abc import Sequence
from dataclasses import dataclass

Vector = list[float]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EmbeddingDocument:
    text: str
    title: str | None = None


class EmbeddingProvider(ABC):
    name: str
    model: str

    @abstractmethod
    async def embed_documents(self, documents: Sequence[EmbeddingDocument]) -> list[Vector]:
        """Embed KB chunks, returned in input order."""

    @abstractmethod
    async def embed_query(self, query: str) -> Vector:
        """Embed a user question (query and document embeddings may use different prompts)."""

    async def aclose(self) -> None:  # noqa: B027 - optional hook, no-op by default
        """Release network resources."""
def fingerprint(
    documents: list[Document], config: ChunkingConfig, embedder: EmbeddingProvider
) -> str:
    digest = hashlib.sha256()
    digest.update(f"v{INDEX_FORMAT_VERSION}|{embedder.name}:{embedder.model}|".encode())
    digest.update(json.dumps(asdict(config), sort_keys=True).encode())
    for doc in sorted(documents, key=lambda d: d.doc_id):
        digest.update(f"|{doc.doc_id}|{doc.title}|{doc.lang}|".encode())
        digest.update(doc.body.encode())
    return digest.hexdigest
… [220 more characters omitted]

class CountingEmbedder(FakeEmbedder):
    def __init__(self, model: str = "fake-embed") -> None:
        super().__init__(model=model)
        self.documents_embedded = 0

    async def embed_documents(self, documents: Sequence[EmbeddingDocument]) -> list[Vector]:

        return await super().embed_documents(documents)

// Same marker syntax the backend parses (app/rag/citations.py): [1], [2, 3], [1][3].
const MARKER = /\[(\d+(?:\s*,\s*\d+)*)\]/g;

export const citationAnchor = (messageId: string, n: number) => `cite-${messageId}-${n}`;

/**
 * Turns [n] markers into Markdown links (#cite-<message>-<n>) so the renderer can show them
 * as clickable chips. Numbers without a matching source are left as plain text.
 */
export function linkCitations(answer: string, messageId: string, known: Set<number>): string {
  return answer.replace(MARKER, (whole, group: string) => {
    const numbers = group.split(",").map((n) => Number(n.trim()));
    if (!numbers.every((n) => known.has(n))) return whole;
    return numbers.map((n) => `[${n}](#${citationAnchor(messageId, n)})`).join("");
  });
}
  question: "How long are backups kept?",
  ...overrides,
});

## Speed

### End to end with Claude (17 LLM turns)

| | p50 | p95 |
|---|---|---|
| Time to first token | 2.6 s | 9.9 s |
| Full answer | 4.8 s | 11.5 s |
| Low-score refusal (no LLM call) | 0.8 s | – |

Tokens per answer: about 1,360 input (38% served from the prompt cache) and about 275 output. At the list prices for `claude-opus-5` ($5 / $25 per million input/output tokens, cache reads about 10% of the input price), that is roughly **$0.011 per question**. The whole 18-question benchmark costs about $0.20.

### 🤖 Claude

The "61%" was a similarity score, not answer accuracy: the right document was already ranked first for every question. The source cards now show **high / medium / low relevance** instead, so a correct 0.61 match shows as "high". The part that did get better is the answers: the local model went from 16/18 to **17/18**. Everything is pushed to `dev` as `05869f1`, and CI on PR #2 is green.

**What I tested:**
- **Retrieval:** I compared the current embedding model with three others and one different prompt format. They all rank the right document first for every question. Their higher percentages come from a different score scale, and the fastest alternative is 2–4× slower per query, so I kept `embeddinggemma`.
- **Answers:** I added a prompt rule telling the model to keep the exact numbers and terms from the sources, and to check which row of a threshold table a value falls into.
  - `ministral-3:3b` now passes 17/18. The one remaining miss leaves out the detail "1 request per 200 records".
  - Claude is still at 18/18, with a median answer time of 3.8 s.

**Other changes:**
- **Fingerprint bug fixed:** the index cache now also detects a changed embedding prompt format. Before, only a model change triggered a rebuild. There's a new test for it, and your running stack already rebuilt its index (16 s).
- **Docs:** the embedding comparison and the new results are in `evaluation.md` and the README.
- **Restart:** the stack is rebuilt and running on the new code. I checked a live answer through the browser endpoint and confirmed the new labels are served.

**For you:**
- **Change your `.env`:** it still sets `OLLAMA_CHAT_MODEL=gemma3:4b`, so the running stack still uses that model. Set `ministral-3:3b` there, then run `docker compose up -d backend`.
- **Local answers are still slow**, about 60–75 s each. On this CPU that is the limit, and only a GPU or Claude makes them fast.
- **Free about 12 GB of disk:** the test models are still in the Ollama volume. These are `qwen3.5`, `granite4.2`, `phi4-mini`, `llama3.2`, `qwen3-embedding`, `bge-m3` and `nomic-embed-text`, plus `gemma3:4b` once you've switched. Should I remove them?
- **The two `/export` text files** in the repo root are still untracked and uncommitted.

### 🤖 Claude

I reviewed screenshots of the running UI on desktop and mobile and checked current guidance on chat interfaces. The layout is solid, but it looks like a developer tool rather than a finished product. The good parts are the one centred column, the answer card with numbered sources, and the model and time footer. Here's what I'd change, highest impact first.

### Problems visible in the screenshots
1. **The relevance badge wraps onto two lines** ("high / relevance") because it's squeezed next to the section title. It needs to stay on one line and sit on the right of the card.
2. **The mobile header takes a quarter of the screen.** The title wraps over three lines, then the model selector and the button each take a row. On mobile it should be one row: a short title, and an icon button that opens the model choice and "New conversation".
3. **The citation chips `[1]` are tiny** (16 px) and hard to click. Accessibility guidance asks for at least 24 px, and 44 px for touch.
4. **The keyboard focus outline is removed** from the question box, and the grey footer text is too light to read comfortably. Both are accessibility problems.

### Style and professional look
5. **Brand and typography.** The page uses the system default font, which looks unpolished on Linux. I'd switch to **Inter** with a clear type scale and add a small logo mark next to the title. The colours should come from a few shared variables (brand, neutral, success, warning), which also makes a **dark mode** easy.
6. **Welcome screen.** The dashed box looks like a file drop area. Instead:
   - a centred welcome with the logo and one headline ("Ask the OmniCorp knowledge base")
   - 4 example cards grouped by topic (SSO, API limits, Data and GDPR, Support), each with a small icon
   - the question box placed in the middle of the screen until the first question, as in ChatGPT or Claude
7. **Messages.**
   - The user's message should be a softer shade (light indigo with dark text), not the heavy solid indigo.
   - Answers get a small assistant avatar and label.
   - Under each answer, a row of quiet actions: **Copy**, 👍/👎 (this feeds the planned statistics tab) and **Regenerate**.
8. **Sources.** Show them as compact cards with a document icon, title and section, and the relevance badge on the right. Hide the internal ids like `kb-003#003`, or move them into a tooltip. A "3 sources" summary above the cards would make them easy to scan.
9. **Question box.**
   - one rounded field with the send button inside it, as an arrow icon
   - a **Stop** button while an answer streams; best-practice guides treat this as essential
   - a line underneath: *"Answers come only from internal documentation. Check before sharing with customers."*
10. **Header.**
    - The model selector should show friendly names: "Claude Opus (cloud, fast)" and "Ministral 3B (local, private)".
    - The status line "Ready · 5 documents" should become a small pill-shaped badge.
    - "New conversation" should become an icon button with a tooltip.

### Showing trust (a best practice for AI assistants)
11. **Progress steps while answering:** "Searching the knowledge base → Found 3 sections → Writing the answer". The local model takes about a minute, so visible progress matters most there.
12. **"Not covered" answers:** these should get a clear panel with an icon and a **"Request an SME"** action, which ties in with the human handoff idea in `ideas.md`, rather than a plain amber box.

**My suggestion:** 1–4 are quick fixes. 5–10 are one restyling pass that touches about 4 files, with no backend changes. 11–12 build on the same work.

Should I do the restyle, or first make a clickable mock-up of the new design for you to review before I change the code?

Sources:
- [AI Chat UI Best Practices for 2026 (thefrontkit)](https://thefrontkit.com/blogs/ai-chat-ui-best-practices)
- [Designing AI chat interfaces: anatomy, patterns, pitfalls (Setproduct)](https://www.setproduct.com/blog/ai-chat-interface-ui-design)
- [Chatbot UI Design Patterns and Best Practices 2026 (Fuselab Creative)](https://fuselabcreative.com/chatbot-interface-design-guide/)
- [Designing AI Chatbots: UX Principles for User Trust (Parallel HQ)](https://www.parallelhq.com/blog/ux-ai-chatbots)
- [Chat UI Design in 2026 (UXPin)](https://www.uxpin.com/studio/blog/chat-user-interface-design/)
- [The 20 best looking chatbot UIs in 2026 (Jotform)](https://www.jotform.com/ai/agents/best-chatbot-ui/)

{
  "file_path": "/home/sako/git/ep_kb_rag_chatbot/frontend/src/features/chat/providers.ts",
  "content": "import type { Provider } from \"../../api/types\";\n\n// Readable names for the model picker; unknown models fall back to their raw id.\nconst MODEL_NAMES: [prefix: string, name: string][] = [\n  [\"claude-opus\", \"Claude Opus\"],\n  [\"claude-sonnet\", \"Claude Sonnet\"],\n  [\"claude-haiku\", \"Claude Haiku\"],\n  [\"ministral-3:3b\", \"Ministral 3B\"],\n  [\"qwen3.5:4b\", \"Qwen 3.5 4B\"],\n  [\"gemma3:4b\", \"Gemma 3 4B\"],\n  [\"llama3.2:3b\", \"Llama 3.2 3B\"],\n];\n\nexport function modelName(model: string | null): string {\n  if (!model) return \"Unknown model\";\n  return MODEL_NAMES.find(([prefix]) => model.startsWith(prefix))?.[1] ?? model;\n}\n\n/** \"Claude Opus · cloud\" / \"Ministral 3B · local\". Local models keep data on this machine. */\nexport function providerLabel(p: Provider): string {\n  const where = p.name === \"ollama\" ? \"local\" : \"cloud\";\n  const label = `${modelName(p.model)} · ${where}`;\n  return p.available ? label : `${label} (unavailable)`;\n}\n"
}

### 🤖 Claude

The redesign is running at **http://localhost:8080**, so you can review it in your browser. It's committed and pushed as `b028b51`, but only on the separate **`ui-redesign`** branch; `dev` and PR #2 are unchanged. The 17 frontend tests pass, and I reviewed screenshots on desktop (light and dark) and mobile.

**What changed:**
- **Look:**
  - the Inter font, bundled with the app so it also works offline
  - a logo mark and favicon
  - a consistent colour palette, with **automatic dark mode** that follows the system setting
- **Header:** one compact row on mobile too, with friendly model names ("Claude Opus · cloud", "Ministral 3B · local"), a status badge and an icon button for a new conversation.
- **Welcome screen:** the question box sits in the centre, with four example cards grouped by topic.
- **Answers:**
  - an assistant avatar
  - **progress steps** with a seconds counter while waiting, which helps with the minute-long local answers
  - a **Stop** button while an answer streams
  - **Copy** (without the `[1]` markers, ready to paste into an email) and **Regenerate**
  - a clearer "not covered" panel and error panel
- **Sources:** cleaner cards showing title, section and a match level, with the internal IDs moved into a tooltip.
- **Accessibility:** visible keyboard focus, larger click areas on the citation chips, better text contrast.

**One change of mind:** my earlier high / medium / low labels showed "low relevance" on correct sources. The HTTP 429 question is answered correctly, but its sources only score 0.38–0.42. Every source shown is cited by the answer, so the labels are now **strong match / good match / related**, and none of them sounds negative.

**Not done:**
- **👍/👎 buttons:** the backend has nowhere to store the ratings yet (that's the statistics idea), so the buttons would do nothing.
- **"Request an SME" button:** it would need the human handoff feature.

**Your decision:**
- **Approve:** I merge `ui-redesign` into `dev`, and it becomes part of PR #2.
- **Reject:** I delete the branch, switch back to `dev` and rebuild the frontend, and the UI is exactly as before.

Your local checkout is on `ui-redesign` until then.

### 🤖 Claude

I added the implementation plan to `documentation/taskdocs/ideas.md`, the project's place for feature plans. It isn't committed yet.

The README list has two items that weren't in `ideas.md`: the **MCP server** and the **CLI**. I added them to the list, so the plan covers all nine ideas. It also includes a table showing which parts are already done for each one.

The plan runs in this order:

| Phase | What | Size |
|---|---|---|
| 10 | **Admin foundation + response history:** SQLite storage for each question and answer, a token-protected admin area, History tab with search and CSV export | M |
| 11 | **Statistics:** questions per day, refusal rate, answer times per model, most-cited documents, and a list of refused questions (which shows missing documentation) | S |
| 12 | **Costs:** a price table, cost per question and model, the cache savings, and optimisation hints computed from the data | S–M |
| 13 | **Tests tab:** run the benchmarks from the browser in the background and compare with earlier runs | S |
| 14 | **Detail level + language:** a "More detail" button and a language selector | S |
| 15 | **CLI:** `omnicorp-kb ask "…"` with streamed answers and sources, and an interactive mode | S |
| 16 | **MCP server:** a search tool and an ask tool, usable from Claude Desktop or Claude Code, over stdio and HTTP | M |
| 17 | **Model switches + "Claude only":** runtime switches in the admin area, and embeddings that no longer need Ollama | M–L |
| 18 | **Follow-up questions**, using the stored history | M |

The admin features come first because statistics, costs, history and the tests tab all build on the same storage and admin page. That way the foundation is built once. The biggest infrastructure change, running without Ollama, comes last.

The MCP question is answered in the plan: the chatbot can be offered as an MCP server. Assistants such as Claude Desktop or Claude Code could then search the knowledge base or ask it questions directly, with the same citations and refusals.

Four decisions before starting:
1. **Order:** the admin foundation first, as above, or the quick wins first (phases 14 and 15).
2. **History privacy:** store the full questions and answers with 90-day retention (my recommendation), or only the numbers without text.
3. **Charts:** a small chart library, or simple hand-made charts with no dependency.
4. **"Claude only" embeddings:** run the embedding model inside the backend (my recommendation; no API key, but the image grows by about 1 GB), or use Voyage AI (a paid API key).

Should I commit and push the plan, and which phase should I start with?

<h1 id="assignment-brief">Assignment brief</h1>

Customer Success Managers (CSMs) at OmniCorp Solutions currently spend hours manually searching through hundreds of internal product manuals to answer complex client configuration questions. You have been contracted to build a prototype Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) chatbot that allows CSMs to ask natural language questions and receive accurate, cited answers based strictly on internal documentation.

Your task is to build a full-stack application consisting of a backend API and a frontend chat interface. The backend should ingest a small set of provided text documents (you should create 3-5 mock enterprise knowledge base articles), chunk them, store them in a local or in-memory vector store, and expose a chat endpoint. The frontend must be a web-based UI where users can type questions, view the AI's response, and critically, see the specific document citations used to generate the answer.

As a Lead Engineer, we expect you to focus on system architecture, API design, and operational readiness. The solution should be easy to run locally (e.g., via Docker Compose), well-structured, and include basic tests.
